Java does not really allow this - you cannot place jars inside of jars.\n354.                   You must either provide all the dependency jars to the user (usually lib/\n355.                   under the directory containing the runnable jar) or explode all the jars\n356.                   and repackage them into a single jar. The problem is that while class files\n357.                   are nicely organized into the package namespace and should not collide, the\n358.                   META-INF directories of the jars will collide. Maven's standard assembly\n359.                   plugin does not account for this and will just clobber metadata. This then\n360.                   causes runtime errors, particularly with Spring. Instead, we use the shade\n361.                   plugin which has transformers that will for example append files of the same\n362.                   name rather than overwrite them in the combined JAR. NB: Don't use a version\n363.                   of the shade plugin older than 1.3.2, as it fixed MSHADE-76 (files not merged\n364.                   properly if some input files are missing a terminating newline) -->\n365.                 <groupId>org.apache.maven.plugins</groupId>\n366.                 <artifactId>maven-shade-plugin</artifactId>\n367.
